By definition.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/ordering/proj/iso).

Suppose
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=(n,v_{1},E_{1})
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=(n,v_{2},E_{2})
.
If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
f_{1}
is a topological ordering of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, then there exists a topological ordering
f_{2}
of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
v_{1}\circ f_{1}=v_{2}\circ f_{2}
.

Proof.

We have some bijection
g
from
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
such that
v_{2}=v_{1}\circ g
since
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
To this end, we choose
f_{2}=g^{-1}\circ f_{1}
.
This is a bijection from
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
, and for any
(n_{1},n_{2})\in E_{2}
,
f_{2}^{-1}(n_{1})<f_{2}^{-1}(n_{2})
since
f_{1}^{-1}(g(n_{1}))<f_{1}^{-1}(g(n_{2}))
.
So,
f_{2}
is a topological ordering of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Lastly, we have
v_{2}\circ g^{-1}=v_{1}
from
v_{2}=v_{1}\circ g
, and then
v_{2}\circ f_{2}=v_{2}\circ g^{-1}\circ f_{1}=v_{1}\circ f_{1}
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/ordering/span).

If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
f
is a topological ordering of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
f
is also a topological ordering of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

Proof.

By the assumption, we have some
(n,v,E_{1})=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
(n,v,E_{2})=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
where
E_{1}\subseteq E_{2}
.
We know
f
is a bijection between
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
, and
f^{-1}(n_{1})<f^{-1}(n_{2})
for any
(n_{1},n_{2})\in E_{2}
.
To this end, it suffices to show that
f^{-1}(n_{1})<f^{-1}(n_{2})
for any
(n_{1},n_{2})\in E_{1}
, which actually holds since
E_{1}\subseteq E_{2}
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/traceable/join).

\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is traceable if and only if both
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
are traceable.

Proof.

This is a corrorally of
subsection C.1
and
subsection C.1
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/ordering/multi/iso).

If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
has more than one topological ordering, then
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
has more than one topological ordering.

Proof.

Let
(n,v_{1},E_{1})=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
(n,v_{2},E_{2})=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we have some bijection
g
such that
E1=\{\,(g(n_{1}),g(n_{2})\mid(n_{1},n_{2})\in E_{2})\,\}
and
v_{1}\circ g=v_{2}
.
Assuming
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are the given topological orderings of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, we can see that
g^{-1}\circ f_{1}
and
g^{-1}\circ f_{2}
are topological orderings of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and they are distinct since
f_{1}\neq f_{2}
.

To this end, let’s see
g^{-1}\circ f_{1}
is a topologial ordering of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We can easily see
g^{-1}\circ f_{1}
is a bijection between
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
since
g
and
f_{1}
are bijections between
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<n}
.
Let
(n_{1},n_{2})\in E_{2}
.
We have
f_{1}^{-1}(g(n_{1}))<f_{1}^{-1}(g(n_{2}))
since
(g(n_{1}),g(n_{2}))\in E_{1}
and
f_{1}
is topological ordering of
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
So,
(g^{-1}\circ f_{1})^{-1}(n_{1})<(g^{-1}\circ f_{1})^{-1}(n_{2})
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/traceable/iso).

If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is traceable, then
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is traceable.

Proof.

This follows by the contraposition of
subsection C.1
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/ordering/multi/span).

If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
has more than one topological ordering, then
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
has more than one topological ordering.

Proof.

This immediately follows by
subsection C.1
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/traceable/span).

If
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is traceable, then
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is traceable.

Proof.

This follows by the contraposition of
subsection C.1
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/ordering/union/multi).

If
0<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
and
0<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
, then
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
has more than one topological ordering.

Proof.

We have the following topological orderings of
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.1
.

\displaystyle f(n)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}f_{1}(n)&(0\leq n<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})\\
f_{2}(n-|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})+|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}&(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}\leq n<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})\end{cases}

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(n)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}f_{2}(n)+|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}&(0\leq n<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})\\
f_{1}(n-|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})&(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}\leq n<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})\end{cases}

To this end, we show
f\neq f^{\prime}
.
We consider the following three cases.

-
Case
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}=|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
.

We have some
n^{\prime}<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
such that
f_{2}(n^{\prime})=0
since
f_{2}
is a bijection between
\mathbb{N}_{<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}}
and
\mathbb{N}_{<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}}
and
0<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
.
Now we can see
f(n^{\prime})=f_{1}(n^{\prime})
and
f^{\prime}(n^{\prime})=|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
.
That implies
f(n^{\prime})\neq f^{\prime}(n^{\prime})
since
f_{1}(n^{\prime})<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
.
As a result
f\neq f^{\prime}
.

-
Case
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
.

We can see
f(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})=f_{2}(0)+|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
and
f^{\prime}(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})=f_{2}(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})+|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
.
This implies
f(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})\neq f^{\prime}(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet})
since
f_{2}
is a bijection, and
0<|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}
.
As a result
f\neq f^{\prime}
.

-
Case
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}>|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}
.

We can show
f(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})\neq f^{\prime}(|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet})
in a similar way to the case above. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(graph/traceable/union).

\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is traceable if and only if

(1)

|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}=0
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is traceable, or

(2)

|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}=0
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is traceable.

Proof.

We show each direction.

•

\Rightarrow
By the contraposition of
subsection C.1
,
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}=0
or
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}=0
hold.
If
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}|_{\bullet}=0
,
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
holds.
So, the first case of the goal follows by
subsection C.1
.
The case for
|\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}|_{\bullet}=0
can be shown similary.

•

\Leftarrow
If the first case holds,
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. So the goal follows by
subsection C.1
. Another case is similar. ∎

C.2.
Properties for environment

\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

and

\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

\mathcal{G}

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/in/binding).

b_{{\mathrm{1}}}\in b_{{\mathrm{2}}}
if and only if
b_{{\mathrm{1}}}=b_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

We will prove each direction.

-
Case
\Rightarrow
.

We show the contraposition.
So, suppose
b_{{\mathrm{1}}}\neq b_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We have
\#_{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(b_{{\mathrm{2}}})=0
by definition.
Thus,
b_{{\mathrm{1}}}\notin b_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

-
Case
\Leftarrow
.

We have
\#_{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(b_{{\mathrm{2}}})=1
by definition.
Thus,
b_{{\mathrm{1}}}\in b_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. ∎

by

.

subsection C.2
.

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/intrp/num/bindings/ord).

Let
[\![\Gamma]\!]=(n,v,E);\mathbb{S}
.
Then,
\#_{b}(\Gamma)=|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\mid v(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|
for any
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\Gamma
.

-
Case
\Gamma=\cdot
.

In this case,
\#_{b}(\Gamma)=0
.
On the other hand,
n=0
.
So, the right hand of the goal is also
0
.

-
Case
\Gamma=b^{\prime}
.

We consider whether
\mathtt{unr}\,b^{\prime}
or
\mathtt{ord}\,b^{\prime}
.

-
SCase
\mathtt{unr}\,b^{\prime}
.

In this case,
\#_{b}(\Gamma)=0
and
[\![\Gamma]\!]=(0,\emptyset,\emptyset);\{b^{\prime}\}
, which implies
|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\mid v(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|=0
.

-
SCase
\mathtt{ord}\,b^{\prime}
.

In this case,

(H.1
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma]\!]=(1,\{0\mapsto b^{\prime}\},\emptyset);\emptyset.

We consider wether
b^{\prime}=b
.

-
SSCase
b^{\prime}=b
.

\#_{b}(\Gamma)=1
by definition, and
|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\mid v(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|=1
by (H.1).

-
SSCase
b^{\prime}\neq b
.

\#_{b}(\Gamma)=0
by definition, and
|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\mid v(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|=0
by (H.1).

-
Case
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Let
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]=(n_{1},v_{1},E_{1});\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]=(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2});\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We have

(H.2
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle\#_{b}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})=|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n_{1}}\mid v_{1}(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|

(H.3
[3.2]
)

\displaystyle\#_{b}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})=|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n_{2}}\mid v_{2}(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|

by the induction hypothesis.
We also have

(H.4
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle n=n_{1}+n_{2}

(H.5
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle v(n)=\begin{cases}v_{1}(n)&\text{if $0\leq n<n_{1}$}\\
v_{2}(n-n_{1})&\text{if $n_{1}\leq n<n_{1}+n_{2}$}\end{cases}

by definition.
From those,
|\{\,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{<n}\mid v(n^{\prime})=b\,\}|=\#_{b}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\#_{b}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\#_{b}(\Gamma)
.

-
Case
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/in/equiv).

Suppose
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Then,
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
if and only if
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
By definition, we have

(H.1
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.2
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}.

We consider wether
\mathtt{unr}\,b
or
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

-
Case
\mathtt{unr}\,b
.

The goal follows by
subsection C.2
and (H.2).

-
Case
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

The goal follows by
subsection C.1
,
subsection C.2
, and (H.1).

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/in/sup).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

Proof.

Let
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
By definition, we have

(H.1
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.2
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\supseteq\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We consider wether
\mathtt{unr}\,b
or
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

-
Case
\mathtt{unr}\,b
.

We have
b\in\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So,
b\in\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by (H.2).
Consequently,
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
follows by
subsection C.2
.

-
Case
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

This case follows by
subsection C.1
,
subsection C.1
,
subsection C.2
, and (H.1). ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/equiv/runtime).

Suppose
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is a runtime environment if and only if
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is a runtime environment.

Proof.

We show the contraposition of each direction.
If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is not a runtime environment, we have
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
for some
x\mathord{:}S
.
So,
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
follows by
subsection C.2
, which implies
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is not a runtime environment.
Another direction is similar.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/sub/runtime).

If
C\lesssim\Gamma
, then
\Gamma
is a runtime environment.

Proof.

We prove the goal by contraction.
Assuming
\Gamma
is not a runtime environment, we have some
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma
.
So,
x\mathord{:}S\in C
by
subsection C.2
.
However, this contradicts the fact
C
is a runtime environment.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/equiv/ctx).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\simeq\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{G}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=[]
.

Immediately by the assumption.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
.

We have

(H.1
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\simeq\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]

by IH.
Let

(H.2
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.3
[2.3]
)

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.4
[2.4]
)

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma^{\prime}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime};\mathbb{S}^{\prime}.

We have

(H.5
[2.5]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.6
[2.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by (H.1), (H.2), and (H.3).
Then, we have

(H.7
[2.7]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}*\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}

by
subsection C.1
and
subsection C.1
.
Furethermore,

(H.8
[2.8]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}.

From (H.7) and (H.8), we have the goal
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}],\Gamma^{\prime}\simeq\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}],\Gamma^{\prime}
by definition.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Similar to the case above.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\parallel\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma^{\prime}\parallel\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
except we use
subsection C.1
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/sub/ctx).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\lesssim\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{G}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=[]
.

Immediately by the assumption.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Let

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma^{\prime}]\!]=\mathfrak{G};\mathbb{S}.

Then, we have

(H.1
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.2
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\supseteq\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by IH.
What we need to show is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}*\mathfrak{G}

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}\supseteq\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\cup\mathbb{S}

for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We choose
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}*\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
.
The first goal follows by
subsection C.1
,
subsection C.1
, and (H.1).
The second goal follows by (H.2).

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Similar to the case above.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\parallel\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma^{\prime}\parallel\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}
except we use
subsection C.1
and
subsection C.1
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/replace/ord).

Let
b\in\Gamma
,
[\![\Gamma]\!]=\mathfrak{G};\mathbb{S}
,
[\![\Gamma^{\prime}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime};\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
, and
\mathtt{ord}\,b
, then
[\![\Gamma[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\Gamma
.

-
Case
\Gamma=\cdot
.

Vacuously true since
b\in\Gamma
cannot hold.

-
Case
\Gamma=b^{\prime}
.

In this case
b^{\prime}=b
since
b\in\Gamma
is supposed.
Then,
\mathfrak{G}=(1,{0\mapsto b},\emptyset)
and
\mathbb{S}=\emptyset
by definition.
That implies
\mathfrak{G}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
.
Consequetly, the goal
[\![\Gamma[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=[\![\Gamma^{\prime}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
holds.

-
Case
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
We have
\mathfrak{G}=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by definition.
We consider the following cases.

-
SCase
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

We have
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
by the induction hypohtesis.
Then,
[\![\Gamma[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
by definition.
To this end, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]=(\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}})[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]
by
subsection C.1
.

-
SCase
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

We have
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
by the induction hypothesis.
On the other hand,
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Then,
[\![\Gamma[\Gamma^{\prime}/b]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by definition.
To this end, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]=(\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}*\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}})[\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}/b]
by
subsection C.1
.

-
SCase
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case above.

-
SCase
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Vacuously true since
b\in\Gamma
.

-
Case
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case above except we use
subsection C.1
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/remove/equiv).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}
.

Proof.

We consider wether
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}

-
Case
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Consequently, the goal directly follows by the assumption.

-
Case
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
We have

(H.1
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.2
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by the assumption.
Now we consider whether
\mathtt{unr}\,b
or not.

-
SCase
\mathtt{unr}\,b
.

We have
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\setminus\{b\}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\setminus\{b\}
by
subsection C.2
.
So, the goal follows by (H.1) and (H.2).

-
SCase
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

We have
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[[\![\cdot]\!]/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[[\![\cdot]\!]/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.1
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/remove/sub).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
We have

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\supseteq\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by the assumption.
Now, we consider wether
\mathtt{unr}\,b
or not.

-
Case
\mathtt{unr}\,b
.

We have

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\setminus\{b\}

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\setminus\{b\}

by
subsection C.2
.
So, the goal follows by (H.1) and (H.2).

-
Case
\mathtt{ord}\,b
.

We consider whether
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
or not.

-
SCase
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
b\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
We have

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[[\![\cdot]\!]/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[[\![\cdot]\!]/b];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by
subsection C.2
.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.1
,
subsection C.1
, (H.1), and (H.2).

-
SCase
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
b\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-b}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-b}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Consequently, the goal directly follows by the assumption.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/replace/equiv).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathtt{ord}\,T
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]\simeq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
,
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
, and
\mathfrak{G};\mathbb{S}=[\![\Gamma]\!]
.
Then, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by definition.
We consider if
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}

-
Case
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So,
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}/x\mathord{:}T];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\mathbb{S}
and
[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}/x\mathord{:}T];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\cup\mathbb{S}
by
subsection C.2
.
To this end, it suffices to show
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathfrak{G}/x\mathord{:}T]\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\mathfrak{G}/x\mathord{:}T]
, which follows by
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
x\mathord{:}T\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}T\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}T]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Now the goal directly follows by the assumption.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/replace/runtime/sub).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathtt{ord}\,T
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}T]\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[C/x\mathord{:}T]
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
By definition we have
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\supseteq\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We consider wether
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

-
Case
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}T\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}T]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

\displaystyle[\![\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}[C/x\mathord{:}T]]\!]=\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T];\mathbb{S}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by
subsection C.2
.
So, to this end, we get some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T]\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T]
.
We choose
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T]
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[[\![C]\!]/x\mathord{:}T]
.
The condition follows by
subsection C.1
and
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
x\mathord{:}T\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}T\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So, the goal directly follows by the assumption.

∎

l\notin\mathtt{dom}(C)

.

Routine by structural induction on
C
.

subsection C.2
.

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/intrp/order/ctx).

|[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}=|[\![\mathcal{C}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{C}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=[]
.

In this case,

\displaystyle|[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle|[\![\mathcal{C}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}

by definition.
So, we can see the goal holds.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case,

\displaystyle|[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle|[\![\mathcal{C}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}

by definition.
Now, we have
|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}=|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}
by the induction hypothesis, by which we can have the goal.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

These cases are similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/intrp/traceable/ctx).

If
[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]
is traceable, then
[\![C]\!]
is traceable.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{C}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=[]
.

This case follows by the assumption.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]*[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]

is traceable.
Then, we know
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]
is traceable by
subsection C.1
.
Now, we can have the goal by the induction hypothesis.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case

\displaystyle[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]\cup[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]

is traceable.
So, we have the following cases by
subsection C.1
.

-
SCase
|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
.

We have
|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
by
subsection C.3
.
So,
[\![C]\!]=[\![\cdot]\!]
by definition, which is trivially traceable.

-
SCase
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]
is traceable.

We can have the goal by the induction hypothsis.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. ∎

Remark

.

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/order-defined/equiv).

Suppose
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Then,
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is order-defined if and only if
C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is order-defined.

It suffices to show

[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
is traceable if and only if
[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
is traceable

for any
l
.
We have
\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}\simeq\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}
by
subsection C.3
.
Then, the goal follows by
subsection C.1
.
∎

[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
is traceable

for any

.

by

.
Then, the goal follows by
subsection C.1
and
subsection C.1
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/usage/equiv).

Suppose
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
are traceable.
If
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\simeq C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Proof.

Let
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
be the unique topological orderings of
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
, respectively.
Let
(n_{1},v_{1},E_{1})=[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2})=[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
By definition, we have
n_{1}=n_{2}
.
Moreover, we have some topological ordering
f_{2}^{\prime}
of
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
by
subsection C.1
such that
v_{1}\circ f_{1}=v_{2}\circ f_{2}^{\prime}
.
Since
C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is order-defined,
f_{2}^{\prime}=f_{2}
, and thus,
v_{1}\circ f_{1}=v_{2}\circ f_{2}
.
This means
\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/usage/sub).

Suppose
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
are traceable.
If
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Proof.

Let
(n_{1},v_{1},E_{1})=[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2})=[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
By definition, we have
(n_{1},v_{1},E_{1})\simeq\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathrel{<_{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2})
for some
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=(n_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime},E_{1}^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=(n_{2}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime})
, where
n_{1}=n_{1}^{\prime}=n_{2}^{\prime}=n_{2}
and
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
.
Here
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
are also traceable by
subsection C.1
.
Let
f_{1}
be the unique topological ordering of
(n_{1},v_{1},E_{1})
.
By
subsection C.1
, we have some topological ordering
f_{1}^{\prime}
of
(n_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime},E_{1}^{\prime})
such that
v_{1}\circ f_{1}=v_{1}^{\prime}\circ f_{1}^{\prime}
.
By
subsection C.1
,
f_{1}^{\prime}
is also a topological ordering of
(n_{2}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime})
.
By
subsection C.1
again, we have some topological ordering
f_{2}^{\prime}
of
(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2})
such that
v_{2}^{\prime}\circ f_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}\circ f_{2}^{\prime}
.
Knowing
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
, we have

(H.1
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle v_{1}\circ f_{1}=v_{2}\circ f_{2}^{\prime}

Since, a topological ordering of
(n_{2},v_{2},E_{2})
is unique by definition.
(H.1) implies
\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/equiv/null/ctx).

If
\mathcal{C}[C]\simeq\cdot
, then
C\simeq\cdot
.

Proof.

We have
[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]\simeq[\![\cdot]\!]
by definition.
So,
|[\![\mathcal{C}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
by
subsection C.3
.
From that, we can see
|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
, which implies
[\![C]\!]\simeq[\![\cdot]\!]
, and thus,
C\simeq\cdot
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/runtime/intrp/traceable/ctx/replace).

Suppose
C\not\simeq\cdot
.
If
[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]
and
[\![C^{\prime}]\!]
are traceable, then
[\![\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
is traceable.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{C}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=[]
.

This case immediately follows by the assumption
[\![C^{\prime}]\!]
is traceable.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case,
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]*[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
is traceable by definition.
So,
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
are tracebale by
subsection C.1
.
We have
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
is traceable by the induction hypothesis.
Therefore,
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]]\!]*[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]=[\![\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
is traceable by
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case,
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]\cup[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
is traceable by definition.
So, we have the following cases by
subsection C.1
.

-
SCase
|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
is traceable.

We have
|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
by
subsection C.3
.
That implies
|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
, and thus
[\![C]\!]=[\![\cdot]\!]
.
Therefore,
C\simeq\cdot
, but, this contradicts to the assumption
C\not\simeq\cdot
.
So, this case is vacuously true.

-
SCase
|[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
and
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]
is traceable.

We have
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
is traceable by the induction hypothesis.
So,
[\![\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]]\!]=[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]]\!]\cup[\![C]\!]
is traceable by
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/ordering/pop).

If
l\mathord{:}[m],C
is order-defined, then
C
is order-defined.

Proof.

We show

if
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable, then
[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable

for any
l^{\prime}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]=[\![l\mathord{:}[m]]\!]*[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
So, we can have
[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable by
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]=[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
So, the goal follows by the assumption.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/ordering/push).

If
C
is order-defined, then
l\mathord{:}[m],C
is order-defined.

Proof.

We show

if
[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable, then
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable

for any
l^{\prime}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]=[\![l\mathord{:}[m]]\!]*[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.1
.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]=[\![\langle C\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
So, the goal follows by the assumption.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/usage/concat).

\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\overline{C_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Proof.

By definition, we have some topological ordering
f
of
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
.
From that, we have topological orderings
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
of
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
such that
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
by
subsection C.1
.
By the way, each topological ordering of
[\![C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\!]
and
[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]
is unique.
So, the goal follows by definition.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(env/usage/replace/ctx).

Suppose
C\not\simeq\cdot
.
If

(H.1
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{C}[C]}=m

(H.2
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\overline{C}=m_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.3
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle\overline{C^{\prime}}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},

then there exist
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
m_{{\mathrm{3}}}
such that

(1)

m=m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{3}}}
and

(2)

\overline{\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]}=m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{C}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=[]
.

In this case,
m=m_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
So, we choose
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\varepsilon
and
m_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\varepsilon
, which clearly
satisfy the condition in the goal.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case,
m=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
by
subsection C.3
.
We have some
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}
such that

(H.4
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}

(H.5
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}

by the induction hypothesis.
To this end, we choose
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
m_{{\mathrm{3}}}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
We can see

\displaystyle m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{3}}}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=m,

and

\displaystyle m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{3}}}=m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]}\odot\overline{C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\overline{\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]}

by
subsection C.3
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case,
|[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
or
|[\![C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
because if the orders of both graphs are greater than zero
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
is undefined by
subsection C.1
.
By the way, for any
C
, we have
[\![C]\!]\simeq[\![\cdot]\!]
by
|[\![C]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
, which implies
C\simeq\cdot
by definition.
So, it suffices to consider the following cases.

-
SCase
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]\simeq\cdot
.

We have
C\simeq\cdot
by
subsection C.3
.
This contradicts to the assumption
C\not\simeq\cdot
.
So, this case is vacuously true.

-
SCase
C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\simeq\cdot
.

We have
\mathcal{C}[C]\simeq\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]
and
\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]\simeq\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]
.
We know
[\![\mathcal{C}[C]]\!]
and
[\![\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
are traceable.
So,
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\!]
and
[\![\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]]\!]
are traceable by
subsection C.1
.
So,
\overline{\mathcal{C}[C]}=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]}
and
\overline{\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]}=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}]}
. by
subsection C.3
.
Now, the goal follows by the induction hypothesis.

-
Case
\mathcal{C}=C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

∎

C.4.
Properties for typing

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/env/remove/well-formed).

If
\Gamma
is well-formed, then
\Gamma^{-b}
is well-formed.

Proof.

We can see
\#_{b^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-b})\leq\#_{b^{\prime}}(\Gamma)
for any
b^{\prime}
.
So, the goal follows by the aassumption.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/env/hole/runtime/well-formed).

If
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}T]
is well-formed, then
\mathcal{G}[C]
is well-formed.

Proof.

We can see
\#_{y\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G}[C])\leq\#_{y\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}T])
for any
y\mathord{:}S
since
C
has no variable bindings.
So, the goal follows by the assumption.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/env/in/ord/unique).

If
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
is well-formed and
\mathtt{ord}\,S
, then
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}
.

Proof.

By definition
\#_{x\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S])=\#_{x\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G})+1
.
So,
\#_{x\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G})=0
since
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
is well-formed and
\mathtt{ord}\,S
.
That implies the goal
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/env/replace/ord).

If
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
is well-formed and
\mathtt{ord}\,S
, then
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma/x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}[\Gamma]
.

Proof.

We know
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}
by
subsection C.4
.
So,
\mathcal{G}[\Gamma/x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}
, by which the goal follows.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/closed).

If
\Gamma\vdash M:T\mid e
, then
\mathtt{fv}(M)\subseteq\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)
.

Proof.

Routine by induction on the given derivation.
We use
subsection C.2
in the case
(T-Weaken)
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/inv).

unit

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{unit}:T\mid e
, then
\Gamma\lesssim\cdot
and
T=\mathtt{Unit}
.

, and

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/canonical).

unit

If
C\vdash V:\mathtt{Unit}\mid e
, then
V=\mathtt{unit}
.

resource

If
C\vdash V:[m]\mid e
, then
V=l
for some
l
.

arrow

If
C\vdash V:S\rightarrow_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e
, then

•

V=\mathtt{new}_{m}
for some
m
,

•

V=\mathtt{op}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
for some
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,

•

V=\mathtt{split}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}},m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
for some
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
m_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,

•

V=\mathtt{drop}
, or

•

V=\lambda x.M
for some
x
and
M
.

uarrow

If
C\vdash V:S\rightarrowtriangle_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e
, then
V=\lambda^{\circ}x.M
for some
x
and
M
.

rarrow

If
C\vdash V:S\twoheadrightarrow_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e
, then
V=\lambda^{>}x.M
for some
x
and
M
.

larrow

If
C\vdash V:S\rightarrowtail_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e
, then
V=\lambda^{<}x.M
for some
x
and
M
.

uprod

If
C\vdash V:S\otimes T\mid e
, then
V=V_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

oprod

If
C\vdash V:S\odot T\mid e
, then
V=V_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on
V
.
We drop unsuitable cases by
subsection C.4
.
∎
