Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/subst/void).

If
\Gamma\vdash M:T\mid e
and
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)
, then
\Gamma\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e
for any
V
.

Proof.

It suffices to show
M=M[V/x]
.
We have
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(M)
by
subsection C.4
and the assumptions.
Therefore,
M=M[V/x]
follows by
Appendix C
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/subst/unr).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash M:T\mid e

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma

(H.3
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle\cdot\vdash V:S\mid 0,

then
\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e
.

Proof.

By induction on the given derivation of (H.1).

-
Case
(T-Unit)
,
(T-New)
,
(T-Op)
,
(T-Split)
, and
(T-Close)
.

In these cases
\Gamma=\cdot
.
So, (H.2) cannot hold.

-
Case
(T-Loc)
.

In this case,
\Gamma=l\mathord{:}[m]
.
So, (H.2) cannot hold.

-
Case
(T-Var)
.

In this case,

(H.4
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma=x^{\prime}\mathord{:}T

(H.5
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle M=x^{\prime}

(H.6
[1.3]
)

\displaystyle e=0.

We have
x=x^{\prime}
and
S=T
by
subsection C.2
, (H.2), and (H.4).
Now the goal
\cdot\vdash V:S\mid 0
is identical to (H.3).

-
Case
(T-Abs)
.

In this case,

(H.7
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle M=\lambda x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.8
[3.2]
)

\displaystyle T=S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.9
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle e=0

(H.10
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)

(H.11
[3.3.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathtt{unr}\,\Gamma

(H.12
[3.5]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma,x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}.

We have
x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\neq x
by (H.2) and (H.10).
So, we have

(H.13
[3.6]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S},x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

by the induction hypothesis, and

\displaystyle(\lambda x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}})[V/x]=\lambda x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x].

It also clearly holds that

\displaystyle\mathtt{unr}\,\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}

\displaystyle x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S})

by (H.11) and (H.10), respectively.
So we can derive the goal

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash\lambda x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid 0

by
(T-Abs)
, where
\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}
is well-formed because we know
\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S},x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is well-formed by (H.13).

-
Case
(T-UAbs)
,
(T-RAbs)
,
(T-LAbs)
.

These cases are similar to the case
(T-Abs)
.

-
Case
(T-App)
.

In this case,

(H.14
[4.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.15
[4.2]
)

\displaystyle M=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.16
[4.3]
)

\displaystyle e=e^{\prime}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.17
[4.4]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:S^{\prime}\rightarrow_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.18
[4.5]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S^{\prime}\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}.

We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
or
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by (H.2) and (H.14).
So, it suffices to consider the following three cases.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

We have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S^{\prime}\rightarrow_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:S^{\prime}\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by the induction hypothesis.
So, we can derive the goal
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:T\mid e^{\prime}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
(T-App)
, where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}=(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{-x\mathord{:}S}
is well-formed by
subsection C.4
and (H.1).

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

We have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S^{\prime}\rightarrow_{e^{\prime}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

by the induction hypothesis for (H.17).
We have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
, but this contradicts to the well-formedness of
\Gamma(=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
So, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:S^{\prime}\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by applying
subsection C.4
to (H.18).
We also know
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
So, we can derive the goal by
(T-App)
, where the environment is well-formed by
subsection C.4
and (H.1).

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the sub case above.

-
Case
(T-UApp)
,
(T-RApp)
,
(T-LApp)
,
(T-UPair)
, and
(T-OPair)
.

These cases are similar to the case
(T-App)
.

-
Case
(T-ULet)
.

In this case,

(H.19
[5.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma=\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.20
[5.2]
)

\displaystyle M=\mathtt{let}\,x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes x_{{\mathrm{2}}}=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,N

(H.21
[5.5]
)

\displaystyle\{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}\uplus\{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}])

(H.22
[5.3]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.23
[5.4]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash N:T\mid e.

First of all, we can see
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by (H.2) and (H.21).
So,

(H.24
[5.5.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]^{-x\mathord{:}S}=\mathcal{G}^{-x\mathord{:}S}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}].

We also note that

(H.25
[5.8]
)

\displaystyle\{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}\uplus\{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}^{-x\mathord{:}S}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}])

by (H.21).
We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
or
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}
by
subsection C.2
, (H.2), and (H.19).
So, it suffices to consider the following three cases.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have

(H.26
[5.6]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.27
[5.7]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{-x\mathord{:}S}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash N[V/x]:T\mid e,

by the induction hypothesis and (H.24).
As a result, we can derive the goal by
(T-ULet)
, (H.25), (H.26), and (H.27), where the environment is well-formed by
subsection C.4
.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}
.

We have

(H.28
[5.9]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

by the induction hypothesis.
We have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G})
because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G})
, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
, but this contradicts to the well-formedness of
\Gamma(=\mathcal{G}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}])
.
Moreover, since
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}])
.
So, we have

(H.29
[5.10]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash N[V/x]:T\mid e

by
subsection C.4
and (H.23).
We also know
\mathcal{G}^{-x\mathord{:}S}=\mathcal{G}
.
As a result, we can derive the goal by
(T-ULet)
, (H.25), (H.28), and (H.29), where the environment is well-formed by
subsection C.4
.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}
.

Similar to the sub cases above.

-
Case
(T-OLet)
.

Similar to the case
(T-ULet)
.

-
Case
(T-Weaken)
.

In this case,

(H.30
[6.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M:T\mid e^{\prime}

(H.31
[6.2]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.32
[6.3]
)

\displaystyle e^{\prime}\leq e.

We consider wether
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
or not.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e^{\prime}

by the induction hypothesis.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
That is because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
.
Under the hypothesis, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\Gamma
by
subsection C.2
and (H.31).
However, this contradicts the well-formedness of
\Gamma
since
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma
.
Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e^{\prime}

by
subsection C.4
and (H.30), and, in this case,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}
.

So, in either case, we can have

(H.33
[6.4]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e^{\prime}.

We also have

(H.34
[6.5]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}^{-x\mathord{:}S}

by
subsection C.2
and (H.31).
Now we can derive the goal by
(T-Weaken)
, (H.32), (H.33), and (H.34); and
\Gamma^{-x\mathord{:}S}
is well-formed by
subsection C.4
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/subst/ord).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]\vdash M:T\mid e

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathtt{ord}\,S

(H.3
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash V:S\mid 0

then
\mathcal{G}[C]\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e
.

Proof.

By induction on the given derivation of (H.1).

-
Case
(T-Unit)
,
(T-New)
,
(T-Op)
,
(T-Split)
, and
(T-Close)
.

In these cases, we have
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]=\cdot
.
So,
x\mathord{:}S=\cdot
by
subsection C.2
(
)
, but this cannot happen.

-
Case
(T-Loc)
.

In this case, we have
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]=l\mathord{:}[m]
.
So,
x\mathord{:}S=l\mathord{:}[m]
by
subsection C.2
(
)
, but this cannot happen.

-
Case
(T-Var)
.

In this case,

(H.4
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]=y\mathord{:}T

(H.5
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle M=y

(H.6
[1.3]
)

\displaystyle e=0.

We have

(H.7
[1.4]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=[]

(H.8
[1.5]
)

\displaystyle x\mathord{:}S=y\mathord{:}T

by
subsection C.2
(
)
and (H.4).
So,
x=y
and
S=T
by (H.8).
Now, the goal is identical to (H.3).

-
Case
(T-Abs)
.

In this case, we know
\mathtt{unr}\,\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
.
However, this contradicts to
\mathtt{ord}\,S
.
So, this case cannot happen.

-
Case
(T-UAbs)
.

In this case,

(H.9
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle M=\lambda^{\circ}x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.10
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle T=T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rightarrowtriangle_{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.11
[2.3]
)

\displaystyle e=0

(H.12
[2.4]
)

\displaystyle x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S])

(H.13
[2.5]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]\parallel x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:T_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}.

We have

(H.14
[2.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[C]\parallel x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:T_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

by the induction hypothesis.
We have

(H.15
[2.7]
)

\displaystyle x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}[C])

by (H.12) since
\mathtt{dom}(C)
has no variable.
Therefore, we can derive

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[C]\vdash\lambda^{\circ}x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:T_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rightarrowtriangle_{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

by
(T-UAbs)
, (H.14), and (H.15).
We can see
\mathcal{G}[C]
is well-formed because
\mathcal{G}[C]\parallel x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}T_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is well-formed by (H.14), and
\lambda^{\circ}x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]=(\lambda^{\circ}x_{{\mathrm{1}}}.M_{{\mathrm{1}}})[V/x]
because we have
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by (H.12).

-
Case
(T-RAbs)
and
(T-LAbs)
.

Similar to the case
(T-UAbs)
.

-
Case
(T-App)
.

In this case,

(H.16
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.17
[3.2]
)

\displaystyle M=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.18
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle e=e_{{\mathrm{0}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.19
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:S\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.20
[3.5]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}.

There are three cases by
subsection C.2
(
)
and (H.16).

-
SCase
\mathcal{G}=[]
and
x\mathord{:}S=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

This case cannot happen since
x\mathord{:}S=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is impossible.

-
SCase
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have

(H.21
[3.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

by the induction hypothesis for (H.19).
Since we know
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S],\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is well-formed by (H.1),
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by
subsection C.4
and (H.2).
From that, we have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
, but this contradicts to the well-formedness of
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S],\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
So, we have

(H.22
[3.7]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:S\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by
subsection C.4
and (H.20).
Now we can derive the goal

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C],\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash(M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}})[V/x]:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{0}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by
(T-App)
, (H.21), and (H.22), where the environment is well-formed by
subsection C.4
.

-
SCase
\mathcal{G}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{2}}}[x\mathord{:}S]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the sub case above.

-
Case
(T-UApp)
,
(T-RApp)
,
(T-LApp)
,
(T-UPair)
, and
(T-OPair)
.

Similar to the case
(T-App)
.

-
Case
(T-ULet)
.

In this case,

(H.23
[4.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.24
[4.2]
)

\displaystyle M=\mathtt{let}\,x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes x_{{\mathrm{2}}}=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.25
[4.3]
)

\displaystyle\{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}\uplus\{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}])

(H.26
[4.4]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.27
[4.5]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid e.

We have

\displaystyle x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}

\displaystyle x\neq x_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by (H.25).
We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
by
subsection C.2
.
We know
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
is well-formed by (H.1).
From those, we have
\#_{x\mathord{:}S}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}])=1
since
\mathtt{ord}\,S
.
So, we have the following cases by
subsection C.2
.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have some
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have

(H.28
[4.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

by the induction hypothesis for (H.26).
We can see
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}])
because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}])
, we have
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
since
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
x\mathord{:}S\notin\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, but this contradicts to the well-formedness of
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
since
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
So, we have

(H.29
[4.7]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:T\mid e

by
subsection C.4
and (H.27).
We can see

(H.30
[4.8]
)

\displaystyle\{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}\uplus\{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]])

by (H.25) since
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S]]
and
C
has no variable bindings.
Now, we can derive

(3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]\vdash\mathtt{let}\,x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes x_{{\mathrm{2}}}=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:T\mid e

by
(T-ULet)
, (H.28), (H.29), and (H.30).
To this end, we will show

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]=\mathcal{G}[C].

Then,
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]
is well-formed by
subsection C.4
and (H.1), and the derived judgment matches to the goal.
We have

\displaystyle g[x:S][C/x:S]=g1[g1^{\prime}[x:S]][C/x:S]

by (H.23).
Since,
\mathtt{ord}\,S
, we have
\mathcal{G}[C]=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]]
by applying
subsection C.4
to both sides.

-
SCase
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have some
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that

(H.31
[4.9]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]

by
subsection C.2
.
Now, we have

(H.32
[4.10]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid e

by the induction hypothesis for (H.27).
Since,
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S]
is well-formed and
\mathtt{ord}\,S
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S][C/x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C]

by
subsection C.4
.
From that and (H.31), (H.32) results in

(H.33
[4.11]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid e

since
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
x\neq x_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We have
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
because if
x\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, we have some
x\mathord{:}S^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
for some
S^{\prime}\neq S
since
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
in this case, but this contradicts the well-formedness of
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
since
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
So, we have

(H.34
[4.12]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

by
subsection C.4
and (H.26).
We have

(H.35
[4.13]
)

\displaystyle\{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}\uplus\{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}])

by (H.25) since
C
has novariable bindings.
Now, we can derive

(4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\vdash\mathtt{let}\,x_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes x_{{\mathrm{2}}}=M_{{\mathrm{1}}}[V/x]\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}[V/x]:T\mid e

by
(T-ULet)
, (H.33), (H.34), and (H.35).
To this end, we will show

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]=\mathcal{G}[C].

Then,
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
is well-formed by
subsection C.4
, and the derived judgment matches the goal.
We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S][C/x\mathord{:}S]=\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}][C/x\mathord{:}S]

by (H.23).
Since
x\mathord{:}S\notin\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, the right hand side equals to
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C/x\mathord{:}S][\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
.
Since
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
is well-formed and
\mathtt{ord}\,S
, the left hand side equals to
\mathcal{G}[C]
by
subsection C.4
.

-
Case
(T-OLet)
.

Similar to the case
(T-ULet)
.

-
Case
(T-Weaken)
.

In this case,

(H.36
[6.1]
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.37
[6.2]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]\lesssim\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.38
[6.3]
)

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq e.

We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have
x\mathord{:}S\in\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by
subsection C.2
, (H.2), and (H.37).
From that, we get some
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[x\mathord{:}S]=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by
subsection C.2
.
Now, we can have

(H.39
[6.4]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[C]\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

by the induction hypothesis.
To this end, we will show
\mathcal{G}[C]\lesssim\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[C]
.
Using that, we can show the goal by
(T-Weaken)
, (H.39), and (H.38).

Remember that we have had
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]\lesssim\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[x\mathord{:}S]
.
So,
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S][C/x\mathord{:}S]\lesssim\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[x\mathord{:}S][C/x\mathord{:}S]
by
subsection C.2
and (H.2).
Here, we know
\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[x\mathord{:}S]
are well-formed by (H.1) and (H.36).
Consequently, we have
\mathcal{G}[C]\lesssim\mathcal{G}^{\prime}[C]
by applying
subsection C.4
to both sides.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(typing/subst).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]\vdash M:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{G})

(H.3
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash V:S\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}},

then
\mathcal{G}[C]\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on whether
\mathtt{unr}\,S
or not (namely
\mathtt{ord}\,S
).

-
Case
\mathtt{unr}\,S
.

We have
C\lesssim\cdot
and
\cdot\vdash V:S\mid 0
by
subsection C.4
and (H.3).
We have
x\mathord{:}S\in\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S]
by
subsection C.2
.
So, we have
(\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S])^{-x\mathord{:}S}\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by
subsection C.4
and (H.1).
We can see
(\mathcal{G}[x\mathord{:}S])^{-x\mathord{:}S}=\mathcal{G}[\cdot]
from (H.2).
We have
\mathcal{G}[C]\lesssim\mathcal{G}[\cdot]
by
subsection C.2
.
Since
e_{{\mathrm{1}}}<e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we can derive the goal by
(T-Weaken)
.

-
Case
\mathtt{ord}\,S
.

We have
C\vdash V:S\mid 0
by
subsection C.4
and (H.3).
Now we have
\mathcal{G}[C]\vdash M[V/x]:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}
by
subsection C.4
.
Since
e_{{\mathrm{1}}}<e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we can derive the goal by
(T-Weaken)
.

∎

C.5.
Properties for heap typing

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/weaken).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{H}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\lesssim C_{{\mathrm{2}}},

then
C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

We will show the following facts, by which
C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathcal{H}
follows since
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

(1)

C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is order-defined.
We have
C_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is order-defined by (H.1).
So, this follows by
subsection C.3
.

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
This follows by
subsection C.2
.

(3)

\overline{\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}}=\overline{\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}}
for any
l
.
We have shown
[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
and
[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
are traceable.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.3
. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/track/new).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\vdash\mathcal{H}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}),

then
\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\}
.

Proof.

We show each condition of the heap typing for the goal.

(1)

\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]
is order-defined.
We will show
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable for any
l^{\prime}
.
By
subsection C.1
, it suffices to show the property for some isomorphic graph to
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
l^{\prime}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
since
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})
by (H.1) and we have (H.2).
So, we have
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]\simeq[\![l\mathord{:}[m]]\!]
.
A topological ordering of
[\![l\mathord{:}[m]]\!]
is a bijection from
\mathbb{N}_{<1}
to
\mathbb{N}_{<1}
, which is unique.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]\simeq[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
.
Here, a topological ordering of
\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
is unique since
\mathcal{C}[\cdot]
is order-defined by (H.1).

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\})

By definition,
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})\cup\{l\}
and
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\})=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
.
So, the subgoal holds.

(3)

For any
l^{\prime}
,
n
,
m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\})(l^{\prime})=(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime\prime})
,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1
and
m^{\prime\prime}\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
n=0
,
m_{{\mathrm{0}}}=m
, and
m^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon
.
Furthermore,
l^{\prime}\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
.
So, we have
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}\simeq l\mathord{:}[m]
.
Consequently, what we need show are

\displaystyle|[\![l\mathord{:}[m]]\!]|_{\bullet}=1

\displaystyle\varepsilon\odot m\leq m.

The first subgoal follows by the definition.
The second subgoal follows by the OPM properties.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\})(l^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}(l^{\prime})
, and
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
.
So the subgoal follows by the assumption
\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/track/split).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq m^{\prime},

then
\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}
.

Proof.

We show each condition of the heap typing for the goal.

(1)

\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]
is order-defined.
We have

(H.3
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\text{ is order-defined}

by (H.1).
We will show
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
is traceable for any
l^{\prime}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]
.
We can see
[\![l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\!]
is traceable.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.3
because
\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]
is traceable by (H.3).

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]
.
Here,
\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]
is traceable because
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]
is traceable by (H.3).

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})
.
We have
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})
by (H.1).
So,
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})\cup\{l\}=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
by definition.
From that, the subgoal holds.

(3)

For any
l^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})=(n^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime\prime})
,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n^{\prime}+1
and
m^{\prime\prime}\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}\leq m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
We consider wether
l\neq l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
n^{\prime}=n+1
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}=m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}=m
.
So, what we need to show are

\displaystyle|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1+1

\displaystyle m\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}.

We have

(H.4
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1

(H.5
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle m\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}

by the assumption.
We have
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+2
and
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[\cdot]]\!]|_{\bullet}+1
by
subsection C.3
.
So, the first subgoal follows by (H.4).

We have some
m_{{\mathrm{01}}}
and
m_{{\mathrm{03}}}
such that

(H.6
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}}=m_{{\mathrm{01}}}\odot m^{\prime}\odot m_{{\mathrm{03}}}

(H.7
[3.5]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l}}=m_{{\mathrm{01}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{03}}}

by
subsection C.3
.
Now the second subgoal follows by (H.2), (H.5), (H.6), (H.7), and the properties of OPM.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})
, and
\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{1}}}],l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}\simeq\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
.
So, the subgoal follows by (H.1).

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/track/op).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq m^{\prime},

then
l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\}
.

Proof.

We show each condition of the heap typing for the goal.

(1)

l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C
is order-defined.
We know
l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C
is order-defined by (H.1).
So, this case follows by
subsection C.3
and
subsection C.3
.

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\})
.
By definition,
\mathtt{dom}(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)=\{l\}\cup\mathtt{dom}(C)
and
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\})=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
.
We have
\{l\}\cup\mathtt{dom}(C)=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
by (H.1).
So, the subgoal holds.

(3)

For any
l^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\})(l^{\prime})=(n^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime\prime})
,
|[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n^{\prime}+1
and
m^{\prime\prime}\odot\overline{\langle(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}\leq m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
n^{\prime}=n
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}=m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
,
m^{\prime\prime}=m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
|[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=1+|[\![\langle C\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}
, and
\overline{\langle(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}=m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\overline{\langle C\rangle_{l}}
.
So, what we need to show are

\displaystyle|[\![\langle C\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n

\displaystyle m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\overline{\langle C\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}.

We have
|[\![\langle(l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C)\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1
by (H.1).
So, the first subgoal follows by definition.
We have
m\odot\overline{\langle(l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C)\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
by (H.1) too.
So,

(H.3
[3.2]
)

\displaystyle m\odot m^{\prime}\odot\overline{\langle C\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}

So, the second subgoal follows by (H.2), (H.3), and the properties of OPM.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\})(l^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})
, and
\langle(l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle(l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C)\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
.
So, the subgoal follows by (H.1).

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/track/cl1).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq m^{\prime},

then
\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}
.

Proof.

We show each condition of the heap typing for the goal.

(1)

\mathcal{C}[\cdot]
is order-defined.
We know

(H.3
[1.1]
)

\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]
is order-defined

by (H.1)
We will show
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
, which equals to
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]]\!]
by definition, is traceable for any
l^{\prime}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

For this
l^{\prime}
, we know
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]]\!]
is traceable by (H.3).
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.3
.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

For this
l^{\prime}
, we know
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]]\!]
is traceable by (H.3).
That is equivalent to the goal.

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})
.
We have
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})\cup\{l\}=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
by (H.1).
So, it suffices to show
l\in\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
.
We have
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1+1
by (H.1).
So,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1
by
subsection C.3
.
That implies
l\in\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
.

(3)

For any
l^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})=(n^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime\prime})
,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n^{\prime}+1
and
m^{\prime\prime}\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}\leq m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
We consider whether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

In this case,
n^{\prime}=n
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}=m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}=m
.
So, what we need show are

\displaystyle|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1

\displaystyle m\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}.

We have

(H.4
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle|[\![\langle(\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]])\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n+1+1

(H.5
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle m\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}

by (H.1).
So, the first subgoal follows by
subsection C.3
and (H.4).
We have some
m_{{\mathrm{01}}}
and
m_{{\mathrm{02}}}
such that

(H.6
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]}=m_{{\mathrm{01}}}\odot m^{\prime}\odot m_{{\mathrm{02}}}

(H.7
[3.5]
)

\displaystyle\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[\cdot]}=m_{{\mathrm{01}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{02}}}

by
subsection C.3
.
So, the second subgoal follows by (H.2), (H.5), (H.6), and (H.7).

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})
, and
\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
.
So, the subgoal follows by (H.1) ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/track/cl2).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}),

then
\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

We show each condition of the heap typing for the goal.

(1)

\mathcal{C}[\cdot]
is order-defined.
We know

(H.3
[1.1]
)

\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]
is order-defined

by (H.1)
We will show
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]
, which equals to
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]]\!]
by definition, is traceable for any
l^{\prime}
.
We consider wether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

For this
l^{\prime}
, we know
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]]\!]
is traceable by (H.3).
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.3
.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

For this
l^{\prime}
, we know
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l^{\prime}}[\cdot]]\!]
is traceable by (H.3).
That is equivalent to the goal.

(2)

\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})
.
We have
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})\cup\{l\}=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})\cup\{l\}
by (H.1), and
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})
by (H.2).
So, it suffices to show
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
.
We have
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=1
by (H.1).
So,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=0
by
subsection C.3
.
That implies
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})
.

(3)

For any
l^{\prime}
,
n^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
, and
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathcal{H}(l^{\prime})=(n^{\prime},m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime\prime})
,
|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n^{\prime}+1
and
m^{\prime\prime}\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}}\leq m^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
We consider whether
l^{\prime}=l
or not.

-
Case
l^{\prime}=l
.

This case cannot happen since
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})
.

-
Case
l^{\prime}\neq l
.

In this case,
\mathcal{H}(l^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\})(l^{\prime})
, and
\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]]\rangle_{l^{\prime}}
.
So, the subgoal follows by (H.1) ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/par).

If
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}
, then
\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
.

Proof.

We show the contraposition.
So, suppose
\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})\neq\emptyset
.
Then, we have some
l
,
m
, and
m^{\prime}
such that
l\mathord{:}[m]\in C_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}]\in C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
By definition, we can see
0<|[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}
and
0<|[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}
.
So,
[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]\cup[\![\langle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rangle_{l}]\!]
has more than one topological ordering by
subsection C.1
, which results in
[\![\langle(C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}})\rangle_{l}]\!]
has more than one topological ordering.
Now
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}]\!]
has more than one topological ordering by the contraposition of
subsection C.3
, which implies
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
is not order-defined.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(heap/par2).

If
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}
and
\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
, then
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

We will show the following facts, by which
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}
follows since
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}
.

•

\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
is order-defined.
Since
\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
, we can observe
\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}\simeq\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}
for any
l
by
subsection C.3
,
subsection C.2
, and
subsection C.2
.
So, a topological ordering of
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}]\!]
is unique by
subsection C.1
.
That implies the subgoal.

•

\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}])=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}])
.
We have
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\lesssim\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
by
subsection C.2
(
)
and
subsection C.2
.
So, the subgoal follows by
subsection C.2
.

•

\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}}=\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}}
for any
l
.
Since
\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
, we can observe
\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}\simeq\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}
for any
l
by
subsection C.3
,
subsection C.2
, and
subsection C.2
.
We know
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}]\!]
is traceable since
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
is order-defined, and so
[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\rangle_{l}]\!]
is traceable by
subsection C.3
.
Now we can have the subgoal by
subsection C.3
.

∎

C.6.
Properties for preservation

Remark

.

by

,

, and
subsection C.2
.

.

by

given

.

Case

,
(RE-RBeta)
, and

Similar to the case
(RE-UBeta)
.

-
Case

by

twice given

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt

[1.5]

by

,

Case

-
Case
(RC-Op)
.

, which must have an effect.

So, this case is vacuously true. ∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(subj/cmp/effect).

[0.1]

\displaystyle(\{l\}\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C))\cap\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset

\displaystyle l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash l:[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\mid e

\displaystyle l\mathord{:}[m_{{\mathrm{2}}}],C^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\}.

Having
\mathtt{dom}(C)=\{l\}
by
subsection C.2
and (H.8), we can easily see the first subgoal.
We can derive the second subgoal by
(T-Loc)
,
(T-Weaken)
, and (H.11).
As for the third subgoal, we have

\displaystyle C,C^{\prime\prime}\lesssim l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C^{\prime\prime}

by
subsection C.2
and (H.8).
So,

\displaystyle l\mathord{:}[m^{\prime}],C^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m)\}

by
subsection C.5
, (H.3), and (H.6).
Then, the subgoal follows by
subsection C.5
and (H.10).

-
Case
(RC-Ne)
,
(RC-Cl1)
,
(RC-Cl2)
, and
(RC-Sp)
.

In these cases, we can derive
C\vdash M:T\mid 0
.
So, the goal follows by
subsection C.6
given
\mathcal{C}=[],C^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(subj/cmp/noeffect/ctx).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle M\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow_{\gamma}M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash\mathcal{E}[M]:T\mid 0

(H.3
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C]\vdash\mathcal{H},

then there exists
C^{\prime}
such that
(\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C})=\emptyset
,
C^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{E}[M^{\prime}]:T\mid 0
, and
\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}]\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{E}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=[]
.

This case directly follows by
subsection C.6
.

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case, we have

(H.4
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle C\lesssim C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.5
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M]:S\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.6
[2.3]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(H.7
[2.4]
)

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{0}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq 0

by
subsection C.4
,
subsection C.2
, and (H.2).
We have
\mathcal{C}[C]\lesssim\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
by
subsection C.2
and (H.4).
So,

(H.8
[2.4.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}

by
subsection C.5
and (H.3).
Here, we notice
\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]=(\mathcal{C}[[],C_{{\mathrm{2}}}])[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
.
Therefore, we can have some
C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that

(H.9
[2.4.2]
)

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot,C_{{\mathrm{2}}}])=\emptyset

(H.10
[2.5]
)

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]:S\rightarrow_{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.11
[2.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

by applying the induction hypothesis to (H.5) and (H.8).
To this end, we choose
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
The subgoals we need to show are

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}})\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot])=\emptyset

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid 0

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}.

Having
\mathtt{dom}(C)=\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C_{{\mathrm{2}}})
by
subsection C.2
and (H.4), we can see the first subgoal follows by (H.9).
We can derive the second subgoal by
(T-App)
,
(T-Weaken)
, (H.6), (H.7), and (H.10).
The third subgoal is identical to (H.11).

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=\mathtt{let}\,x\otimes y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case, we have

(H.12
[3.1]
)

\displaystyle C\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.13
[3.1.1]
)

\displaystyle e\leq 0

(H.14
[3.2]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.15
[3.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[x\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel y\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid e

(H.16
[3.4]
)

\displaystyle\{x\}\uplus\{y\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}])

by
subsection C.4
,
subsection C.2
, and (H.2).
We have
\mathcal{C}[C]\lesssim\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]
by
subsection C.2
and (H.12).
So,

(H.17
[3.5]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}

by
subsection C.5
.
Here we notice that
\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]=(\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}])[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
.
Therefore, we get some
C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that

(H.18
[3.5.1]
)

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[\cdot]])=\emptyset

(H.19
[3.6]
)

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.20
[3.7]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

by applying the induction hypothesis to (H.14) and (H.17).
To this end, we choose
C^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
.
The subgoals we need to show are

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}])\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[\cdot])=\emptyset

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\vdash\mathtt{let}\,x\otimes y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid 0

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]]\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}.

Having
\mathtt{dom}(C)=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}])
by
subsection C.2
and (H.12), we can see the first subgoal follows by (H.18).
We can derive the second subgoal by
(T-ULet)
,
(T-Weaken)
, (H.13), (H.15), (H.16), and (H.19).
The third subgoal is identical to (H.20).

-
Case Other cases.

We can show other cases in a similar way to the cases above.

∎

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=V_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
if
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}
has an unrestricted type.
Otherwise, similar to the case
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}{}^{>}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=\mathtt{let}\,x\otimes y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

In this case, we have

(H.47
[13.1]
)

\displaystyle C\lesssim\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.48
[13.2]
)

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq e

(H.49
[13.3]
)

\displaystyle\{x\}\uplus\{y\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}])

(H.50
[13.4]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.51
[13.5]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[x\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\parallel y\mathord{:}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}

by
subsection C.4
,
subsection C.2
, and (H.2).
We have
C,C^{\prime\prime}\lesssim\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}],C^{\prime\prime}
by
subsection C.2
and (H.47).
So,

(H.52
[13.6]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}],C^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}

by
subsection C.5
and (H.47).
Now, we have some
C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that

(H.53
[13.7]
)

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C_{{\mathrm{1}}}))\cap\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C},C^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset

(H.54
[13.8]
)

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]:S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\otimes S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid 0

(H.55
[13.9]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}],C^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

by
subsection C.6
, (H.1), (H.50), and (H.52).
To this end, we choose
C^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
.
The subgoals we need to show are

\displaystyle(\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}])\setminus\mathtt{dom}(C))\cap\mathtt{dom}(C^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\vdash\mathtt{let}\,x\otimes y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}[M^{\prime}]\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:T\mid e

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}],C^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}.

Having
\mathtt{dom}(C)=\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C_{{\mathrm{1}}}])
by
subsection C.2
and (H.47), we can see the first subgoal follows by (H.53).
We have
\{x\}\uplus\{y\}\uplus\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}])
by (H.49) since
\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{C}[C^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}])
has only locations.
Therefore, we can derive the second subgoal by
(T-ULet)
,
(T-Weaken)
, (H.48), (H.51), and (H.54).
The third subgoal is identical to (H.55).

-
Case
\mathcal{E}=\mathtt{let}\,x\odot y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Similar to the case
\mathcal{E}=\mathtt{let}\,x\otimes y=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,\mathtt{in}\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(subj).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle M\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash M:T\mid e

(H.3
[0.3]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash\mathcal{H},

then there exists
C^{\prime}
such that
C^{\prime}\vdash M^{\prime}:T\mid e
and
C^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the last rule of the given derivation of (H.1).

-
Case
(R-Exp)
.

In this case,

(H.4
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{E}[M_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.5
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle M^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.6
[1.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

(H.7
[1.4]
)

\displaystyle M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\longrightarrow_{\beta}M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.

So, we have
C\vdash\mathcal{E}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]:T\mid e
by
subsection C.6
.
To this end, we choose
C^{\prime}=C
, which satisfies the required conditions.

-
Case
(R-Cfg)
.

In this case,

(H.8
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{E}[M_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.9
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle M^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

(H.10
[2.3]
)

\displaystyle M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow_{\gamma}M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}.

So, we have some
C^{\prime}
such that

(H.11
[2.4]
)

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{E}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}]:T\mid e

(H.12
[2.5]
)

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

by
subsection C.6
.
To this end, we choose
C^{\prime}
as the one obtained above, which has satisfied the required conditions.

∎

C.7.
Properties for progress

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/new).

\mathtt{new}_{m}\,\mathtt{unit}\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for some
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We choose
M^{\prime}=l
such that
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H})
— this is always possible since
\mathcal{H}
is a finite map — and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\}
.
Then, what we need to show is

\displaystyle\mathtt{new}_{m}\,\mathtt{unit}\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow l\mid\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m,\varepsilon)\},

which can be derived by
(RC-Ne)
and
(R-Cfg)
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/split).

If
\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\vdash\mathcal{H}
, then
\mathtt{split}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}},m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for some
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We have some
(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}(l)
by the assumption.
To this end, we choose
M^{\prime}=l\odot l
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}
.
Then, what we need to show is

(8)

\displaystyle\mathtt{split}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}},m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow l\odot l\mid\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}.

Since
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}
, we can derive that by
(RC-Sp)
and
(R-Cfg)
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/op).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle l\mathord{:}[m],C\vdash\mathcal{H}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq m,

then
\mathtt{op}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for some
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition, we have
(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}(l)
such that

(H.3
[1.9]
)

\displaystyle m^{\prime}\odot m\odot\overline{\langle C^{\prime}\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}.

from (H.1).
To this end, we choose
M^{\prime}=l
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime}\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\}
.
Note that
\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}=\mathcal{H}
.
So, what we need to show is

\displaystyle\mathtt{op}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}\longrightarrow l\mid\mathcal{H}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime}\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}})\}.

We have
m^{\prime}\odot m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\overline{\langle C^{\prime}\rangle_{l}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
by (H.2) and (H.3).
So, we can derive the goal by
(RC-Op)
, given
m^{\prime\prime}=m_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\overline{\langle C^{\prime}\rangle_{l}}
, and
(R-Cfg)
.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/close).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[l\mathord{:}[m]]\vdash\mathcal{H}

(H.2
[0.2]
)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq m,

then
\mathtt{drop}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for some
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We have some
(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}(l)
such that

(H.3
[1.1]
)

\displaystyle|[\![\langle\mathcal{C}[\cdot]\rangle_{l}]\!]|_{\bullet}=n

(H.4
[1.2]
)

\displaystyle m^{\prime}\odot\overline{\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle_{l}[l\mathord{:}[m]]}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}

by (H.1).
Let
\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathcal{H}\setminus\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}
.
Note that
\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}=\mathcal{H}
, and
l\notin\mathtt{dom}(\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
We consider whether
n=0
or not.

-
Case
n=0
.

We have
m^{\prime}\odot m\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}
by (H.3) and (H.4).
So, we can see

(H.5
[2.1]
)

\displaystyle m^{\prime}\leq m_{{\mathrm{0}}}

from (H.2).
To this end, we choose
M^{\prime}=\mathtt{unit}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
What we need to show is

(9)

\displaystyle\mathtt{drop}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(0,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}\longrightarrow\mathtt{unit}\mid\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}.

We can derived that by
(RC-Cl2)
,
(R-Cfg)
, and (H.5).

-
Case
n=n^{\prime}+1
for some
n^{\prime}
.

To this end, we choose
M^{\prime}=\mathtt{unit}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n^{\prime},m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}
.
What we need to show is

(10)

\displaystyle\mathtt{drop}\,l\mid\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n^{\prime}+1,m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\}\longrightarrow\mathtt{unit}\mid\mathcal{H}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cup\{l\mapsto(n^{\prime},m_{{\mathrm{0}}},m^{\prime})\},

which can be derived by
(RC-Cl1)
and
(R-Cfg)
.

∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/ctx).

If
M=\mathcal{E}[M_{{\mathrm{1}}}]
and
M_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
, then there exists
M^{\prime}
such that
M\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We get some
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
M_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[M_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
,
M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}]
, and (1)
M_{{\mathrm{2}}}\longrightarrow_{\beta}M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
or (2)
M_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow_{\gamma}M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
by the second assumption.
To this end, we choose
M^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]
.
What we need to show is
\mathcal{E}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[M_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}[\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}]]\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
, which can be derived by using
(R-Exp)
or
(R-Cfg)
depending on whether (1) or (2) holds.
∎

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress/noeffect).

If

(H.1
[0.1]
)

\displaystyle C\vdash M:T\mid 0

(H.2
[0.2]

then

there exist
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
such that
M\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
, or

M=V
for some

.

By induction on the given derivation of (H.1).

(T-Unit)
,
(T-New)
,
(T-Op)
,
(T-Split)
,
(T-Close)
,
(T-Loc)
,
(T-Abs)
,
(T-UAbs)
,

, and

M
is a value in these cases.

This case is vacuously true because

, but this is impossible.

[1.1]

(H.4
[1.1.1]

(H.5
[2.3]

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{0}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcup e_{{\mathrm{2}}}=0

(H.6
[1.2]

(H.7
[2.2]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash M_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S\mid e_{{\mathrm{2}}}.

We have
e_{{\mathrm{0}}}=e_{{\mathrm{1}}}=e_{{\mathrm{2}}}=0
by (H.5).
So, we have the following cases by the induction hypothesis for (H.6).

-
SCase

for some
M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

given
\mathcal{E}=[]\,M_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

SCase
M_{{\mathrm{1}}}=V_{{\mathrm{1}}}
for some
V_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

We have the following cases by the induction hypothesis for (H.7).

-
SSCase

for some
M^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

The first item of the goal follows by

given
\mathcal{E}=V_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,[]
.

M_{{\mathrm{2}}}=V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for some
V_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Remember that we have had

(H.8
[2.5]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash V_{{\mathrm{1}}}:S\rightarrow_{0}T\mid 0

(H.9
[2.6]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash V_{{\mathrm{2}}}:S\mid 0.

We have the following cases by

(
)
and (H.8).

by

and (H.8).

by

(
)
.

.

for some

and
m_{{\mathrm{2}}}
by

for some

by

(

and (H.8).

for some

(

and (H.9).

[2.7]

\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq m

-
Case

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt

,

,

,

,

,

is a value in these cases.

(T-Weaken)
.

In this case,

(H.24
[6.1]
)

\displaystyle C\lesssim C_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(H.25
[6.2]
)

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{1}}}<e

(H.26
[6.3]
)

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash M:T\mid e_{{\mathrm{1}}}.

We have

subsection C.2
and (H.24).
So,
C_{{\mathrm{1}}},C^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{H}
by
subsection C.5
and (H.2).
Consequently, this case follows by the induction hypothesis.

Remark
\the\mythm@cnt
(progress).

If

\displaystyle C\vdash M:T\mid e

\displaystyle C\vdash\mathcal{H},

then

•

M\mid\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
for some
M^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
, or

•

M=V
for some
V
.

Proof.

This follows by
subsection C.7
.
∎
